# ModForge editor text: English
#
# Every word the editor shows is here, one text per line: key = text.
# A translation is a copy of this file with the text after each = in another
# language, named for that language: es.txt, pt-BR.txt, zh-Hans.txt...
# Language > New or update a translation, in the editor, writes one for you
# to start from.
# The texts under [In the game] are the ones the game shows players; the
# rest is the editor. One file does both: put it in the editor's languages
# folder, and in the game's BepInEx\plugins\SMSModForge\Languages folder.
#
# Lines starting with # are notes, and [headings] only help you find your way.
# Neither changes anything.
# Keep {words in braces}, <tags> and [TOKENS:like this] exactly as they are:
# something fills them in or reads them.
# \n starts a new line. An underscore marks the letter Alt reaches a menu by.
# Nothing after the = shows the English. "" shows nothing, on purpose.
# A key ending in .one or .other is one form of a sentence with a number in it.
# A translation has the forms its own language uses; ModForge writes them out.

[This file]

# The language's name, written the way its speakers write it
language.name = English

# yes for a machine translation nobody has checked yet
language.machineTranslated = no

# Who made or checked this translation
language.translators =

[In the game]

# The heading of ModForge's list of packs, on the game's main menu
game.menu.heading = Mods

# Beside ModForge's version on the main menu, in red, when the game is another build than the one ModForge is made for.
game.menu.forgeOtherGame = made for the game's {version}

# In the list of packs on the game's main menu, when there are none
game.menu.noPacks = (no packs detected)

# Beside a pack in the main menu's list: its file would not open
game.pack.unreadable = Could not be read

# Beside a pack in the main menu's list: it was made for another version of the game, {version}
game.pack.otherGameVersion = Incompatible game version ({version})

# Beside a pack in the main menu's list: it needs a newer ModForge than the one installed
game.pack.needsForge = Needs ModForge {version}

# Beside a pack in the main menu's list: it was made with an older ModForge, and should still work
game.pack.olderForge = Built for ModForge {version}

# Beside a pack in the main menu's list: it was made before ModForge wrote its version into packs
game.pack.noForgeVersion = No ModForge version recorded

# Beside a pack in the main menu's list: it is installed in two folders, and {folder} is the one in use
game.pack.installedTwice = Installed twice, using from {folder} folder

# Beside a pack in the main menu's list: it has translations, but none for the language ModForge is being read in, so its own words show
game.pack.notInYourLanguage = Not translated into your language

# Beside a pack in the main menu's list: it changes the same thing of the game's (a character, an object in a level...) as {packs}, other packs in the list, so only one of the two changes shows
game.pack.clashesWith = Changes the same as {packs}

# Under ModForge's list of packs on the game's main menu, when there are two or more. The packs can be dragged up and down; the one lower in the list is loaded later, so its changes show
game.menu.orderHint = Drag a pack to move it. Lower in the list wins when two packs change the same thing.

# The name above the player's own lines in a pack's conversations
game.player = You

# A pack's name, quoted the way the language quotes a name
game.quoted = '{name}'

# Heading of the window shown when a save is loaded that the installed packs might not suit
game.save.title = Before you continue

# The save window, one pack. {pack} is its name, {what} what it changes: "the game's own quests and conversations"
game.save.onePack = {pack} changes {what}, and this save hasn't been played with that yet. Your progress could break - now, or if you remove the pack later.

# The save window, one pack, when the save is older than ModForge's record of what it has been played with
game.save.onePack.cannotTell = {pack} changes {what}, and ModForge can't tell whether this save has been played with that. Your progress could break - now, or if you remove the pack later.

# The save window, several packs; what each changes is listed below it
game.save.somePacks = These packs change the game's own content, and this save hasn't been played with that yet. Your progress could break - now, or if you remove them later.

# The save window, several packs, when the save is older than ModForge's record of what it has been played with
game.save.somePacks.cannotTell = These packs change the game's own content, and ModForge can't tell whether this save has been played with all of it. Your progress could break - now, or if you remove them later.

# The save window: heading of the list of packs
game.save.eachPack = What each pack changes:

# The save window: one pack in the list. {what} is like "quests and conversations"
game.save.packChanges = {pack}: {what}

# The save window: heading of the list of the game's quests the packs change
game.save.questsChanged.one = Quest changed:
game.save.questsChanged.other = Quests changed:

# The save window: one quest in the list, and where the save stands with it
game.save.quest = {quest}: {standing}

# The save window: where the save stands with a quest whose tasks a pack changes
game.save.standing.mayGetStuck = in progress, but a new task was added before where you are - it may get stuck

# The save window: where the save stands with a quest whose tasks a pack changes
game.save.standing.inProgress = in progress, should be fine

# The save window: where the save stands with a quest whose tasks a pack changes
game.save.standing.finished = already finished, so you won't see the changes

# The save window: where the save stands with a quest whose tasks a pack changes
game.save.standing.notStarted = not started yet, fine

# The save window, below the lists
game.save.newGameSafest = Starting a new game is safest.

# The save window: the save holds data of packs that are not running - switched off on the main menu, or not installed. They are listed below it
game.save.missing.one = This save has data from a pack that isn't running now. Nothing is lost: it's kept in the saves you make, and it's all there again when the pack runs.
game.save.missing.other = This save has data from packs that aren't running now. Nothing is lost: it's kept in the saves you make, and it's all there again when they run.

# The same, after the window has already warned about packs that are running
game.save.missingAlso.one = This save also has data from a pack that isn't running now. Nothing is lost: it's kept in the saves you make, and it's all there again when the pack runs.
game.save.missingAlso.other = This save also has data from packs that aren't running now. Nothing is lost: it's kept in the saves you make, and it's all there again when they run.

# The save window: heading over the packs that are installed but unticked on the main menu
game.save.switchedOff = Switched off - tick them on the main menu to run them:

# The save window: heading over the packs that are installed and ticked on the main menu, but could not be loaded
game.save.notLoaded = Installed, but could not be loaded - BepInEx's log says why:

# The save window: heading over the packs that are not installed
game.save.notInstalled = Not installed:

# The save window's buttons
game.save.continue = Continue

# The save window's buttons
game.save.returnToMenu = Return to Main Menu

# The main menu: the small heading over the language flags above Exit. The flags choose the language of the mods - ModForge's own words and the packs - not of the game itself
game.language.flagsHeading = Mod language

# Heading of the window shown after choosing a language that some packs are mostly not translated into
game.language.coverage.title = Not every pack is in {language}

# That window: what it means. {language} is the language just chosen
game.language.coverage.body.one = This pack is less than half translated into {language}. What isn't translated shows in the language the pack was written in.
game.language.coverage.body.other = These packs are less than half translated into {language}. What isn't translated shows in the language each pack was written in.

# That window: heading of the list of packs
game.language.coverage.heading = Translated into {language}:

# That window: a pack in the list, and how much of it is translated
game.language.coverage.share = {pack}: {percent}%

# That window: a pack in the list with no translation into the language
game.language.coverage.none = {pack}: not translated

# That window's buttons: keep the language just chosen, or go back to the one before. {language} is that language
game.language.keep = Keep {language}
game.language.back = Back to {language}

# Heading of the notice, shown once, that packs show the game's own lines in the player's language
game.gameLines.title = The game's own lines

# That notice. {packs} are the packs' names, {language} the player's language
game.gameLines.body.one = {packs} also shows the game's own lines in {language}, in the conversations it changes. If you play the game translated by another mod, the two translations may mix in those conversations.
game.gameLines.body.other = {packs} also show the game's own lines in {language}, in the conversations they change. If you play the game translated by another mod, the two translations may mix in those conversations.

# Tooltip on a tick box

# Tooltip on a tick box
uiOpen.fadeBetweenInvisibleSolid.tip = Fade between invisible and solid. Needs a CanvasGroup, which is added for you.

# Tooltip on a tick box
uiOpen.moveBetweenOwnSize.tip = Move between its own size and the one below. The game uses all of it: (1,0,1) is a flat line - the shop unfolds from one on the way in and folds back to one on the way out - while (0.5,0.5,0.5) is small and (2,2,1) is oversized.

# Tooltip on a text box
uiOpen.03WhatShop.tip = 0.3 is what the shop uses and the commonest in the game.

# Tooltip on a dropdown
uiOpen.curvesGameItselfUses.tip = The curves the game itself uses. QuadInOut is the shop's; the game also uses BounceOut and ElasticOut elsewhere.

# Tooltip on "Play it"
uiOpen.playUiOpenAnimation.tip = Play the animation once in the preview. It is drawn, not applied - the screen is left exactly as it was, and nothing is written to the pack.

[Action and condition fields]

# Option
params.true = True

# Option
params.false = False

# Tooltip on a text
params.whatVariableHolds.tip = What this variable holds.

# Tooltip on "True"
params.true.tip = Set variable to true

# Tooltip on "False"
params.false.tip = Set variable to false

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.variable = Variable

# Tooltip on the 'Variable' field of the SetVariable action
action.setVariable.name.tip = Pack variable to set.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.value = Value

# Tooltip on the 'Value' field of the SetVariable action
action.setVariable.value.tip = New value. Strings round-trip as-is; use 'true' / 'false' for booleans, and numbers in invariant-culture form (e.g. '3.14'). '$varName' copies that variable's current value instead ('$$' escapes a literal dollar). Leave it blank to CLEAR the variable to an empty string — that's kept as a real value, not treated as an unfilled row.

# Tooltip on the 'Variable' field of the IncrementVariable action
action.incrementVariable.name.tip = Numeric pack variable to add to (negative delta subtracts).

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.delta = Delta

# Tooltip on the 'Delta' field of the IncrementVariable action
action.incrementVariable.delta.tip = Amount added to the current value each time the action runs.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.focused = Focused

# Tooltip on the 'Focused' field of the CharacterFocus action
action.setSpriteFocus.focused.tip = When true, every actor's bust in the dialogue is bumped above the CG layer; when false, their resting sorting orders are restored. No per-actor target — it focuses the whole cast, and resets automatically when the dialogue ends.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.actor = Actor

# Tooltip on the 'Actor' field of the LeaveBust action
action.leaveBust.actor.tip = Triggers the vanilla MBase1/Leave fade-out child if present; falls back to an immediate deactivate.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.goPath = GO path

# Tooltip on the 'GO path' field of the SetGameObjectActive action
action.setGameObjectActive.path.tip = A pack overlay/bust name (autocompleted), a vanilla GO name, or a full hierarchy path ('5_Levels/14_Beach/Foreground/Thing'). Inactive objects are found too, so this can activate something that starts disabled.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.active = Active

# Tooltip on the 'Active' field of the SetGameObjectActive action
action.setGameObjectActive.active.tip = true to switch the target on, false to switch it off, or toggle to flip it to the opposite of whatever it currently is. Toggle reads the object's own state, so a parent being hidden around it does not count as off.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.sprite = Sprite

# Tooltip on the 'Sprite' field of the SetSprite action
action.setSprite.sprite.tip = Pack-relative PNG to draw. Applied at the image's own size.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.maskOptional = Mask (optional)

# Tooltip on the 'Mask (optional)' field of the SetSprite action
action.setSprite.mask.tip = Pack-relative PNG for the material's _MaskTex. Leave blank to keep the current mask. Warns if the target has no material or its shader has no mask slot.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.signal = Signal

# Tooltip on the 'Signal' field of the EmitSignal action
action.emitSignal.signal.tip = GC2 signal name (the same string vanilla Trigger components use).

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.delayS = Delay (s)

# Tooltip on the 'Delay (s)' field of the EmitSignal action
action.emitSignal.seconds.tip = Seconds to wait first. 0 sends it straight away, which is what most signals want. Either way the dialogue carries on immediately rather than pausing.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.fromLevel = From level

# Tooltip on the 'From level' field of the TransitionLevels action
action.transitionLevels.fromLevel.tip = Source level (vanilla:<go> or place:<key>).

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.toLevel = To level

# Tooltip on the 'To level' field of the TransitionLevels action
action.transitionLevels.toLevel.tip = Destination level.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.doneSignal = Done signal

# Tooltip on the 'Done signal' field of the TransitionLevels action
action.transitionLevels.signal.tip = Optional signal emitted at the end of the transition.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.totalTimeS = Total time (s)

# Tooltip on the 'Total time (s)' field of the TransitionLevels action
action.transitionLevels.seconds.tip = Total transition length; cross-fade and signal share this budget.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.targetAlpha = Target alpha

# Tooltip on the 'Target alpha' field of the FadeSprite action
action.fadeSprite.to.tip = Target alpha (0..1).

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.durationS = Duration (s)

# Tooltip on the 'Duration (s)' field of the FadeSprite action
action.fadeSprite.seconds.tip = Time over which the alpha tween plays.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.component = Component

# Tooltip on the 'Component' field of the SetComponentProperty action
action.setComponentProperty.component.tip = Which component on the target to write to. A closed list, not a type name: each entry is supported on purpose so the property list below means something and a bad value fails with a readable log line.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.property = Property

# Tooltip on the 'Property' field of the SetComponentProperty action
action.setComponentProperty.property.tip = Which field to set. CanvasGroup: 'alpha' hides the whole UI subtree without deactivating it (so anything animating underneath keeps running); 'interactable' turns its controls on or off; 'blocksRaycasts' decides whether clicks stop here or pass through to what is behind. Hiding a panel usually wants all three, which is three rows.

# Tooltip on the 'Value' field of the SetComponentProperty action
action.setComponentProperty.value.tip = The new value, read to suit the property: a number for 'alpha' (0..1), 'true' / 'false' for the two flags.

# Tooltip on the 'Duration (s)' field of the SetComponentProperty action
action.setComponentProperty.seconds.tip = 0 sets the value immediately; anything higher tweens to it. Numeric properties only — a flag has nothing to tween through, so this is ignored for those and the change lands at once.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.returnToOriginal = Return to original

# Tooltip on the 'Return to original' field of the MoveGameObject action
action.moveGameObject.home.tip = Ease back to the position the object was at before the first MoveGameObject, then release it (so e.g. parallax resumes). Ignores X / Y / Relative — use this for the pan-back at the end of a scene instead of guessing the home coords.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.relative = Relative

# Tooltip on the 'Relative' field of the MoveGameObject action
action.moveGameObject.relative.tip = When true, X/Y are added to the current local position; when false they're absolute.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.x = X

# Tooltip on the 'X' field of the MoveGameObject action
action.moveGameObject.x.tip = World X (offset if 'Relative').

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.y = Y

# Tooltip on the 'Y' field of the MoveGameObject action
action.moveGameObject.y.tip = World Y (offset if 'Relative').

# Tooltip on the 'Duration (s)' field of the MoveGameObject action
action.moveGameObject.seconds.tip = Eased (ease-in + ease-out) move duration. The target is HELD afterward so a parallax effect can't snap it back. 0 = instant snap. Note: world units (e.g. a level pan is y = -17, not -1700).

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.speedS = Speed (°/s)

# Tooltip on the 'Speed (°/s)' field of the SpinGameObject action
action.spinGameObject.speed.tip = Degrees per second around Z. the host mod's a prop uses 1.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.spinning = Spinning

# Tooltip on the 'Spinning' field of the SpinGameObject action
action.spinGameObject.enable.tip = True to start/keep spinning, false to stop.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.musicKey = Music key

# Tooltip on the 'Music key' field of the SwitchMusic action
action.switchMusic.music.tip = A track from the Music tab, or one of the game's own. Both are children of 12_AudioPlayer, so either can be named.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.clip = Clip

# Tooltip on the 'Clip' field of the PlaySFX action
action.playSFX.clip.tip = Pack SFX key (declared on the SFX tab).

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.volume = Volume

# Tooltip on the 'Volume' field of the PlaySFX action
action.playSFX.volume.tip = Playback volume, 0 to 5 - 1 is the game's own level, above it louder. Falls back to the sound's default volume when blank.

# Tooltip on the 'Delay (s)' field of the PlaySFX action
action.playSFX.delay.tip = Seconds to wait before the one-shot plays (action returns immediately).

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.seconds = Seconds

# Tooltip on the 'Seconds' field of the Wait action
action.wait.seconds.tip = How long to pause before the actions after this one run.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition: which transition plays
param.label.transition = Transition

# Label beside a field of an action or condition: how many seconds something stays on screen
param.label.onScreenS = On screen (s)

# Label beside a field of an action or condition: the words shown on a black screen
param.label.screenText = Text

# Tooltip on the 'Transition' field of the Transitions action
action.transitions.style.tip = Which transition plays: the game's own fade to black, blink, white flash or travel fade, or a black screen with words on it, like the game's "A few minutes later...". It covers the dialogue too, and the actions after this one wait until the screen is covered - so whatever they change is not seen changing.

# Tooltip on the 'On screen (s)' field of the Transitions action
action.transitions.seconds.tip = How long the screen stays covered, in seconds, before it clears. The blink and the flash run the game's own course, so they have no time to set.

# Tooltip on the 'Text' field of the Transitions action
action.transitions.text.tip = The words on the black screen - "A few minutes later...", "That night...", anything. Translated with the pack's other texts, and [PV:name] shows a variable.

# One of the transitions the Transitions action plays: the game's fade to black and back
choice.style.fadeToBlack = Fade to black

choice.style.textScreen = Black screen with text

# One of the transitions the Transitions action plays: the game's eyes-closing blink
choice.style.blink = Blink

# One of the transitions the Transitions action plays: the game's white flash
choice.style.whiteFlash = White flash

# One of the transitions the Transitions action plays: the game's fade used when travelling
choice.style.travelFade = Travel fade

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.source = Source

# Tooltip on the 'Source' field of the PickRandomFromList action
action.pickRandomFromList.source.tip = Either a comma-separated literal ('A,B,C'), or '$varName' to read from a String or List pack variable.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.excluding = Excluding

# Tooltip on the 'Excluding' field of the PickRandomFromList action
action.pickRandomFromList.excluding.tip = Optional. Entries to remove from Source before picking — same three shapes as Source ('$varName' is the usual one). This is how you claim a slot nobody else holds: pick from all slots excluding the occupied list.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.target = Target

# Tooltip on the 'Target' field of the PickRandomFromList action
action.pickRandomFromList.target.tip = Pack variable receiving the picked element.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.ifNoneLeft = If none left

# Tooltip on the 'If none left' field of the PickRandomFromList action
action.pickRandomFromList.fallback.tip = Value written to Target when Source is empty or everything was excluded. Use it for an overflow destination with unlimited capacity. Accepts '$varName'. Left blank, Target is cleared (the original behaviour).

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.list = List

# Tooltip on the 'List' field of the AddToList action
action.addToList.list.tip = List-typed pack variable to append to.

# Tooltip on the 'Value' field of the AddToList action
action.addToList.value.tip = Value to append. '$varName' appends that variable's current value instead of the literal text.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.onlyIfAbsent = Only if absent

# Tooltip on the 'Only if absent' field of the AddToList action
action.addToList.unique.tip = When checked, the value is appended only if it isn't already in the list (no duplicates).

# Tooltip on the 'Value' field of the RemoveFromList action
action.removeFromList.value.tip = First matching entry is removed; the action is a no-op when the value isn't present. '$varName' removes that variable's current value — how you free the slot a character is standing on.

# Tooltip on the 'List' field of the ClearList action
action.clearList.list.tip = List-typed pack variable to reset to empty.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.fromList = From list

# Tooltip on the 'From list' field of the CountList action
action.countList.fromList.tip = List-typed pack variable whose entry count is written.

# Tooltip on the 'Target' field of the CountList action
action.countList.name.tip = Pack variable (Int) receiving the number of entries.

param.label.weather = Weather

# Tooltip on the 'Weather' field of the SetWeather action
action.setWeather.weather.tip = Sets today's weather: writes the vanilla rainy-day / snowy-day game variables and refreshes the weather particles on the active level immediately. Clear stops both.

condition.variableEquals.name.tip = Pack variable to read.

# Tooltip on the 'Value' field of the VariableEquals condition
condition.variableEquals.value.tip = Compared as a string (booleans use 'true' / 'false', numbers use the invariant-culture decimal form).

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.threshold = Threshold

# Tooltip on the 'Threshold' field of the VariableGreaterThan condition
condition.variableGreaterThan.value.tip = Passes when the pack variable's numeric value is strictly greater.

# Tooltip on the 'Threshold' field of the VariableGreaterOrEqual condition
condition.variableGreaterOrEqual.value.tip = Passes when the pack variable's numeric value is >= this.

# Tooltip on the 'Threshold' field of the VariableLessThan condition
condition.variableLessThan.value.tip = Passes when the pack variable's numeric value is strictly less.

# Tooltip on the 'Threshold' field of the VariableLessOrEqual condition
condition.variableLessOrEqual.value.tip = Passes when the pack variable's numeric value is <= this.

# Tooltip on the 'Variable' field of the VariableExists condition
condition.variableExists.name.tip = Passes when any value has been written to this pack variable.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.gc2Global = GC2 global

# Tooltip on the 'GC2 global' field of the GameVariableEquals condition
condition.gameVariableEquals.name.tip = GC2 GlobalNameVariable. Vanilla globals (PC, etc.) work via the GNV_ALIASES map.

# Tooltip on the 'Value' field of the GameVariableEquals condition
condition.gameVariableEquals.value.tip = Compared as a string.

# Tooltip on the 'Threshold' field of the GameVariableNumberGreaterThan condition
condition.gameVariableNumberGreaterThan.value.tip = Strictly greater (>).

# Tooltip on the 'Threshold' field of the GameVariableNumberGreaterOrEqual condition
condition.gameVariableNumberGreaterOrEqual.value.tip = Greater or equal (>=).

# Tooltip on the 'Threshold' field of the GameVariableNumberLessThan condition
condition.gameVariableNumberLessThan.value.tip = Strictly less (<).

# Tooltip on the 'Threshold' field of the GameVariableNumberLessOrEqual condition
condition.gameVariableNumberLessOrEqual.value.tip = Less or equal (<=).

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.level = Level

# Tooltip on the 'Level' field of the LevelActive condition
condition.levelActive.level.tip = Level token (vanilla:<go> or place:<key>). Passes when the matching 5_Levels child is currently active in the scene.

# Tooltip on the 'Target' field of the GameObjectActive condition
condition.gameObjectActive.target.tip = The object to test. Category decides how it resolves: a bust, an overlay inside a chosen level, a scene, or a raw GameObject name or hierarchy path. Passes while the resolved object is active in the hierarchy — one that cannot be found reads the same as one switched off, so Negate says "off or absent" rather than "off".

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.key = Key

# Tooltip on the 'Key' field of the InputKey condition
condition.inputKey.key.tip = Unity KeyCode name for the key or mouse button, e.g. Space, Mouse0, F1. Reported by POSITION rather than by the letter printed on the cap, so the letter and number rows land elsewhere on a non-US layout; the picker groups those separately.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.when = When

# Tooltip on the 'When' field of the InputKey condition
condition.inputKey.phase.tip = Pressed and Released are moments, true once per press. Down and Up are states, true for as long as they hold. An edge only means something where the condition is re-checked continuously.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.chance = Chance

# Tooltip on the 'Chance' field of the Random condition
condition.random.chance.tip = DEPRECATED — re-rolls on EVERY evaluation. In a per-frame context (integration rule, dialogue start conditions, button visibility) that means ~60 rolls/second and the probability is meaningless. Switch to 'DailyChance' (once per day) or a LevelRandom variable + comparison.

# Tooltip on the 'Chance' field of the DailyChance condition
condition.dailyChance.chance.tip = Probability of passing, as a whole percentage. 50 = passes on ~half of in-game days. Rolled once per in-game day: the result holds all day, survives save/reload, and changes at each day roll-over. Every DailyChance rolls independently — the console names each one after its dialogue/rule at the day change.

# Tooltip on the 'Variable' field of the VariableStartsWith condition
condition.variableStartsWith.name.tip = Variable whose string value is tested.

# Tooltip on the 'Source' field of the VariableStartsWith condition
condition.variableStartsWith.source.tip = Which store to read: this pack's variables, or the vanilla GC2 globals.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.startsWith = Starts with

# Tooltip on the 'Starts with' field of the VariableStartsWith condition
condition.variableStartsWith.value.tip = Prefix to test for. Check Negate for "doesn't start with". An empty prefix never passes — it would otherwise match everything.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.ignoreCase = Ignore case

# Tooltip on the 'Ignore case' field of the VariableStartsWith condition
condition.variableStartsWith.ignoreCase.tip = Compare case-insensitively. Off by default, matching every other variable comparison.

# Tooltip on the 'List' field of the ListContains condition
condition.listContains.list.tip = List-typed pack variable to search.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.contains = Contains

# Tooltip on the 'Contains' field of the ListContains condition
condition.listContains.value.tip = Entry to look for — an exact, case-sensitive match. Check Negate for "doesn't contain", which is how you express a no-share constraint against an occupancy list.

# Tooltip on the 'List' field of the ListCount condition
condition.listCount.list.tip = List-typed pack variable to count.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.countIs = Count is

# Tooltip on the 'Count is' field of the ListCount condition
condition.listCount.comparison.tip = How the entry count is compared against the value below.

# Tooltip on the 'Value' field of the ListCount condition
condition.listCount.value.tip = Number to compare the entry count against. 'equals 0' is the empty check; negate it for "has anything in it".

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.randomize = Randomize

# Tooltip on the 'Randomize' field of the Timer condition
condition.timer.randomize.tip = Roll a fresh wait in the Min..Max range after every fire, instead of using the fixed 'Wait' value. Use this for wandering / roaming so characters don't move in lockstep.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.waitS = Wait (s)

# Tooltip on the 'Wait (s)' field of the Timer condition
condition.timer.seconds.tip = Real seconds the rule waits between fires. Ignored when 'Randomize' is checked. The gate starts elapsed, so the rule fires once right away and then every interval after.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.minS = Min (s)

# Tooltip on the 'Min (s)' field of the Timer condition
condition.timer.minSeconds.tip = Shortest wait, in real seconds. Only used when 'Randomize' is checked.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.maxS = Max (s)

# Tooltip on the 'Max (s)' field of the Timer condition
condition.timer.maxSeconds.tip = Longest wait, in real seconds. Only used when 'Randomize' is checked. Must be >= Min.

# Label beside a field of an action or condition
param.label.staggerStart = Stagger start

# Tooltip on the 'Stagger start' field of the Timer condition
condition.timer.stagger.tip = Wait a full interval before the FIRST fire instead of firing immediately. Use it when several rules would otherwise start together — one per character, say — so they don't all act on the same frame. Later waits are unaffected: each rule already re-rolls its own interval independently.

condition.weather.state.tip = Raining / Snowing read the vanilla rainy-day / snowy-day game variables; BadWeather passes on either. Check Negate for "clear weather".

# Tooltip on a dropdown
action.whichLevelSTwo.tip = Which of the level's two sprites to act on — the same Back and Front the Places tab authors. Front is sorting order −10, Back is −12. Saved to the pack under the game's own names: Front writes layer=Base, Back writes layer=Secondary.

# Tooltip on a dropdown
action.activateSwitchesDeactivateSwitches.tip = Activate switches it on, Deactivate switches it off, and Toggle flips it to the opposite of whatever it is now. For a Scene, activating also plays its sound - including when a Toggle is what turned it on.

# Tooltip on a text box
action.numberVariableUseOne.tip = A number, or $variable to use one of the pack's variables.

# Tooltip on "− Branch"
action.remove.tip2 = Remove this branch (and its action).

# Tooltip on "+ Add branch"
action.addDiceBranch.tip = Add a weighted branch. New branches are seeded with the chance still missing from 100%.

# Tooltip on a box
gameObject.objectVanillaLevelAlready.tip = An object the vanilla level already has. The pack reaches into it rather than creating it, and anything you don't change here is dropped on save.

# Tooltip on a box
gameObject.differsVanillaObjectS.tip = This differs from the vanilla object it's bound to, so it will be saved. Match the vanilla values again and it drops out.

# Tooltip on "Reset"
gameObject.resetToVanilla.tip = Put this object back to what the vanilla level actually has — the extracted transform, active state and sorting, not zeroes. It then drops out of the manifest again.

# Tooltip on "Copy"
gameObject.copy.tip = Copy this GameObject to the editor clipboard

# Tooltip on "Paste"
gameObject.paste.tip = Paste the clipboard's GameObject as a sibling below this one

# Tooltip on a button
gameObject.remove.tip = Remove this GameObject

# Tooltip on "This GameObject already exists (bind, don't create)"
gameObject.gameobjectAlreadyExistsBind.tip = On: the Name above is looked up in the scene (a bare name or a A > B > C path) and only what you author here is applied to it — the way to modify a vanilla level you don't own. Off: this pack creates the GameObject.

# Tooltip on "Override its transform"
gameObject.overrideTransform.tip = Off (default) leaves the existing object where it is. On applies the Transform row below as LOCAL position / rotation / scale.

# Tooltip on "Override active"
gameObject.overrideActive.tip = Off (default) leaves the object's own active state alone. On applies the Start-active checkbox. Not needed when you're gating it with Active-when conditions.

# Tooltip on a part of the window
gameObject.packRelativePNGPath.tip = Pack-relative PNG path. Leave blank for a pure container GameObject (an empty transform that just groups children).

# Tooltip on a text box
gameObject.worldXPositionLevel.tip = World X position (level centre ≈ 0).

# Tooltip on a text box
gameObject.worldYPositionUp.tip = World Y position (up = positive); e.g. two overlays use 15/18.

# Tooltip on a text box
gameObject.zRotationDegreesPlane.tip = Z rotation in degrees (in-plane spin).

# Tooltip on a text box
gameObject.localXScale1.tip = Local X scale (1 = native; negative mirrors).

# Tooltip on a text box
gameObject.localYScale.tip = Local Y scale.

# Tooltip on a text box
gameObject.spriterendererSortingOrderLower.tip = SpriteRenderer sorting order (lower draws further back).

# Tooltip on "Parallax"
gameObject.parallax.tip = Let this object drift with the mouse. Off (the default) pins it to the level, which is what almost every overlay wants.

# Tooltip on "Start active"
gameObject.startActive.tip = Uncheck for overlays a dialogue reveals later (Flash, Portal).

# Tooltip on a text box
gameObject.initialAlpha01.tip = Initial alpha 0..1 (start at 0 for fade-in overlays like the Portal).

# Tooltip on "Drift with the level"
gameObject.driftLevel.tip = On (the default) keeps the level's own strength, which is what a created object always did. Turn it off to set this object's depth independently.

# Tooltip on a text box
gameObject.depthRelativeLevelS.tip = Depth relative to the level's main sprite, which every vanilla level fixes at 0.75: lower sits further away and lags behind the room, higher sits nearer and overshoots. Vanilla objects use 0.1, 0.5, 0.75 and 1.5.

# Tooltip on "Reversed"
gameObject.reversed.tip = Drift WITH the cursor instead of against it.

# Tooltip on "UI space"
gameObject.uiSpace.tip = Only for an object under a Canvas — it switches the effect to canvas coordinates. A pack's own GameObjects are world sprites, so leave this off unless you're binding to a canvas object.

# Tooltip on a part of the window
gameObject.optionalPackRelativeMask.tip = Optional pack-relative mask PNG bound to _MaskTex, for shader-masked overlays (a cameo).

# Tooltip on "+ Component"
gameObject.addComponent.tip = Attach a generic utility component (fade, disappear, random child, blink) to this GameObject.

# Tooltip on "+ Condition"
gameObject.addActiveCondition.tip = Gate this GameObject on a condition. It switches on while the conditions pass.

# Tooltip on "Switch back off when the conditions stop matching"
gameObject.switchBackOffWhen.tip = Checked (default): the object is active exactly while the conditions hold. Unchecked: it turns on the first time they pass and stays on — a one-way reveal.

# Tooltip on "+ Add NPC child"
gameObject.addNpc.tip = Place an NPC (from the NPCs tab) as a child of this GameObject.

# Tooltip on "+ Add child"
gameObject.addChild.tip = Add a child GameObject nested under this one. Children have the same fields, components, and their own children.

# In place of a GameObject's name when it has none
gameObject.unnamed = (unnamed GameObject)

# Keyboard shortcut shown beside it ("_Export pack")
menu.exportPack.keys = Ctrl+E

# Tooltip on "_Export pack"
menu.exportPack.tip = Save and export straight to where this pack was last exported (asks the first time).

# Keyboard shortcut shown beside it ("Export pack _as…")
menu.exportPackAs.keys = Ctrl+Shift+E

# Tooltip on "Export pack _as…"
menu.exportPackAs.tip = Save and export to a chosen .smspack file; that path becomes the one-click Export target.

# Tooltip on "_Publish for players…"
menu.publishPack.tip = Package this pack as a release: validates it, moves the version, and writes a zip a player extracts straight into the game folder.

# Keyboard shortcut shown beside it ("_Duplicate item")
menu.duplicateItem.keys = Ctrl+D

# Tooltip on "_Duplicate item"
menu.duplicateItem.tip = Duplicate the selected item in the current tab's list (with a fresh key).

# Keyboard shortcut shown beside it ("_Copy item")
menu.copyItem.keys = Ctrl+C

# Tooltip on "_Copy item"
menu.copyItem.tip = Copy the selected list item — paste it into any pack open in this editor session. Ctrl+C works while the list has focus.

# Keyboard shortcut shown beside it ("_Paste item")
menu.pasteItem.keys = Ctrl+V

# Tooltip on "_Paste item"
menu.pasteItem.tip = Paste a copied item of the matching type into the current tab's list. Ctrl+V works while the list has focus.

# Tooltip on "_Rename item…"
menu.renameItem.tip = Rename the selected list item's key (F2 or F12 while the list has focus).

# Keyboard shortcut shown beside it ("De_lete item")
menu.deleteItem.keys = Del

# Tooltip on "De_lete item"
menu.deleteItem.tip = Delete the selected list item (Del while the list has focus).

# Tooltip on "_Themes"
menu.themes.tip = Recolour the editor. Your choice is remembered between sessions.

# Tooltip on "_Confirm changes on save"
menu.confirmChangesSave.tip = Before writing modpack.json, list everything the save will change and ask for confirmation. Your choice is remembered between sessions.

# Tooltip on "_Spell-check node text"
menu.spellCheckNodeText.tip = Underline misspellings using Windows' own spell checker, in the language the lines are written in - the pack's own, or the one you are editing in - both in a node's Text field and down the node list, so mistakes are visible while scanning. A language Windows has no spelling dictionary for is not underlined at all; Windows' language settings can add one. Right-click a squiggle in the Text field for suggestions, to ignore it for this session, or to add the word to your Windows dictionary (the same list Edge and Office use). Turning this off also makes the node list lighter to scroll on very long dialogues. Your choice is remembered between sessions.

# Tooltip on "_Version the pack automatically"
menu.versionPackAutomatically.tip = Move the pack's version on every save: up a minor when the pack gains something, up a patch when something it already had changes. The major number is never moved for you — that one says "this is a new version of the pack", which is yours to decide. Off means the version only ever changes when you type it.

# Tooltip on "Warn when the version goes _backwards"
menu.warnWhenVersionGoes.tip = Ask before saving a version lower than the one already on disk. Going backwards is nearly always a typo, and it makes a new release read as older than the copy a player already has.

# Tooltip on "Warn about _large exports"
menu.warnAboutLargeExports.tip = Ask before exporting a pack folder over 150 MB or 1600 files, in case it has swept up something that is not part of the pack. Unticking clears the per-pack "don't warn again" you may have set from the prompt itself.
# Tooltip on "Offer to _translate before exporting"
menu.offerTranslationOnExport.tip = Before an export or a publish, when some of what players read in this pack is not yet in every language ModForge has, offer to translate it by machine. Only what is still untranslated is sent. For this pack only.

# Tooltip on "Add pack _names to Windows dictionary…"
menu.addPackNamesToDictionary.tip = Put every character, actor, place and NPC name this pack invents into your Windows dictionary in one go, so they stop being flagged as misspellings. Shows you the list first.

# Tooltip on "Check for _updates on start"
menu.checkUpdatesStart.tip = Ask the releases page whether a newer version has been published, once, when the editor starts. Off means no request is made at all. Your choice is remembered between sessions.

# Tooltip on "_Install updates without asking"
menu.installUpdatesWithoutAsking.tip = Skip the what-changed window: a found update downloads and installs itself, saying so in a line at the top of the window rather than opening anything. The editor still restarts to finish, and still asks about an unsaved pack first.

# Tooltip on "_Starmaker Story folder…"
menu.setGameFolder.tip = Where the game is installed, with BepInEx beside it. Only used by the updater, so it can replace the plugin at the same time as the editor - the two are versioned together and a mismatched pair can fail without saying why.

# Tooltip on "Dialogue rows look like the _game"
menu.dialogueRowsLookLike.tip = Draw each line in the node list the way the game draws it — its own font, outline and drop shadow, the speaker's name in their colour, on a dark panel. Costs the spelling squiggles on those rows; they stay in the Text box below the list.

# Tooltip on "Check for updates _now"
menu.checkForUpdates.tip = Ask right now, and answer either way.

# Tooltip on "_Preview quality"
menu.previewQuality.tip = Trade live-preview smoothness for CPU. Your choice is remembered between sessions.

# A preview quality, in the menu
previewQuality.ultra = Ultra

# Tooltip on that preview quality
previewQuality.ultra.tip = 60 fps, 4× anti-aliasing. Smoothest motion, highest CPU.

# A preview quality, in the menu
previewQuality.high = High

# Tooltip on that preview quality
previewQuality.high.tip = 30 fps, 4× anti-aliasing. Same fidelity as Ultra at ~half the CPU.

# A preview quality, in the menu
previewQuality.medium = Medium

# Tooltip on that preview quality
previewQuality.medium.tip = 30 fps, no anti-aliasing. Slight motion-edge shimmer; much lighter.

# A preview quality, in the menu
previewQuality.low = Low

# Tooltip on that preview quality
previewQuality.low.tip = 20 fps, no anti-aliasing. Lightest; choppier motion.

# Tooltip on a part of the window
characters.baseSprite.tip = The bust itself: RGBA PNG, relative to the pack folder. Best at 256x256, the only size whose pixels land exactly as drawn; other sizes are scaled to fit, and a shape that is not square gets transparent bars on two sides.

# Tooltip on "= default"
characters.useDefaultMask.tip = Use the same mask file as the character's default outfit. It is the same file, not a copy — editing this outfit's mask afterwards changes the default outfit's too, so this asks first. For an outfit added to one of the game's characters, it borrows the game's own mask from the bust the game gave them instead; painting over it makes a file of your own.

# Tooltip on a part of the window
characters.whereJiggleAppliesHow.tip = Where the jiggle applies and how strongly. Leave it empty for a bust that does not move. Edit Mask opens the painter.

# Tooltip on "Has Blink frame"
characters.hasBlink.tip = Untick for a bust with no blink art. Blink is not optional by omission — leaving the path empty makes the runtime treat the outfit as broken and skip the whole bust, so this is the way to say the character simply does not blink.

# Tooltip on "= default"
characters.useDefaultMask.tip2 = Use the default outfit's blink. For an outfit added to one of the game's characters, that is the game's own blink from the bust the game gave them, borrowed at the game's full size.

# Tooltip on a part of the window
characters.eyesClosedOverlayUsed.tip = The eyes-closed overlay. Used only while Has Blink frame is ticked.

# Tooltip on "Has Mouth frames"
characters.hasMouth.tip = Tick when the bust has numbered mouth frames, so the mouth animates while the line types.

# Tooltip on "= default"
characters.useDefaultMask.tip3 = Use the default outfit's mouth. For an outfit added to one of the game's characters, that is the game's own four mouth frames from the bust the game gave them, borrowed at the game's full size.

# Tooltip on a part of the window
characters.pathFilenameStartFour.tip = Path and filename start that the four mouth frames share, relative to the pack root — the same kind of path as the Base and Blink rows above, just without the number and the .png. Art/Anna/Mouth loads Art/Anna/Mouth1.png through Mouth4.png.

# Tooltip on "Has Expressions"
characters.hasExpressions.tip = Tick when the bust has expression artwork a line can switch to.

# Tooltip on "= default"
characters.useDefaultMask.tip4 = Use the default outfit's expressions. For an outfit added to one of the game's characters, that is the game's own faces from the bust the game gave them, borrowed at the game's full size.

# Tooltip on a part of the window
characters.pathFilenameStartBust.tip = Path and filename start that this bust's expression files share, relative to the pack root. Art/Anna/Expression loads Art/Anna/ExpressionHappy.png and its siblings — one per face the character has, including any you added.

# Tooltip on "Speed"
characters.speed.tip = How fast the movement cycles. Drives the Bounce and Wave layers; Noise has its own speed.

# Tooltip on "Strength"
characters.strength.tip = How far the pixels travel. Drives the Bounce and Wave layers; Noise has its own strength.

# Tooltip on "Wave frequency"
characters.waveFrequency.tip = How many waves fit down the sprite: low sways, high ripples. Drives the Wave layer only.

# Tooltip on "Noise scale"
characters.noiseScale.tip = How fine the noise grain is. Drives the Noise layer only.

# Tooltip on "Noise speed"
characters.noiseSpeed.tip = How fast the noise drifts. Drives the Noise layer only.

# Tooltip on "Noise strength"
characters.noiseStrength.tip = How much of the noise shows through. Drives the Noise layer only.

# Tooltip on a text box
characters.multipliesBustSColour.tip = Multiplies the bust's colour. Almost always left white.

# Tooltip on "Pixel Snap"
characters.pixelSnap.tip = Rounds movement to whole pixels, matching the game's own look.

# Tooltip on "Default"
characters.resetJiggle.tip = Put this back to the value a new outfit starts with. Nothing else on the row changes.

# Tooltip on "Wet particle active on spawn"
characters.wetParticleActiveSpawn.tip = When checked, the Wet particle system (cloned from Anna_Towel) starts active. Typically enabled only for swim/wet outfit variants.

# Under the preview's size picker, only when the window is too small for the size picked. {shown} and {chosen} are sizes, like 1× and 2×
characters.previewSize.shrunk = Shown at {shown}: the window is too small for {chosen}. It goes back to {chosen} when there is room.

# Tooltip on the preview's size picker
characters.previewSize.tip = How big the bust is drawn. 1× puts one of the preview's pixels on one of the screen's, which is the sharpest it gets; 2× doubles every pixel and is just as exact. The half steps in between have to be resampled, so they look a little softer. The panel grows with it, and the choice is remembered.
# Tooltip on a dropdown
characters.mouthPicker.tip = Show the bust holding one particular mouth frame. Preview only - it changes nothing in the pack.

# Tooltip on "Yapping"
characters.yappingToggle.tip = Animate the mouth in the preview, the way it moves while a line types. Preview only.

# Tooltip on "Breathing"
characters.breathingToggle.tip = Run the idle breathing motion in the preview. Preview only.

# Tooltip on a slider
characters.breathingDepth.tip = Preview-only: how far the bust bobs, in preview pixels. Match it to the game by eye.

# Tooltip on a button
characters.resetBreathingDepth.tip = Put the depth back to its default (4).

# Tooltip on a dropdown
characters.expressionPicker.tip = Show the bust in one particular expression. Preview only - it changes nothing in the pack.

characters.packChanges = This pack changes: {0}

The mod-side loader resolves target names into level indices at load time, so two mods can both add buttons without colliding.

# Box heading
places.placeGameObjects = GameObjects (this place's scene hierarchy)

# Text
places.everythingPlaceBuildsUnder = Everything this place builds under its level, as one hierarchy. A GameObject with a Sprite is a layered sprite object (backdrop, prop, animated layer); leave the Sprite blank for a pure container that just groups children. Every node takes utility components (fade, disappear, random child, blink), nested children, and NPC placements. The NPCs node is the level's own NPCs object — always present, and the home for the NPC hierarchy; its subtree composes local transforms down the chain, so moving a container moves everything under it. Target any node from dialogue by Name with SetGameObjectActive / FadeSprite / MoveGameObject / SpinGameObject. Lower sorting draws further back.

# Text
dialogues.nodeOneChoiceS = This node is one of a Choice's options, so it has no Actions on start. When an option counts as having started is not something the pack can rely on — it may be the moment the menu is drawn rather than the moment the player picks it, which would run the actions for options nobody chose. Put them on Actions on finish, which happens when the option has actually been taken.

# Tooltip on "Also leave on the Talk button (replayable)"
dialogues.alsoLeaveTalkButton.tip = Play on arrival like normal, AND stay on the vanilla Talk button so the player can hear it again — for as long as its start conditions still hold. Without this a dialogue interrupts once and is then gone until its conditions fail and pass again. Greyed out when 'Wait for Talk button' is on, which already leaves it there.

# Tooltip on "Wait for Talk button (no auto-play)"
dialogues.waitTalkButtonNo.tip = Don't start on arrival: the dialogue parks behind the vanilla Talk button, and the player starts it by clicking (with the normal cinematic fade). No jump-scare on level entry. Greyed out when the replay option above is on — that one plays on arrival, which is the opposite of waiting.

# Tooltip on "Queue behind active dialogue"
dialogues.queueBehindActiveDialogue.tip = If another dialogue is playing when this one's conditions pass, wait and start right after it ends. Unchecked (original behavior): it misses that window and only fires when its conditions trigger again while nothing is playing.

# Tooltip on "Set for condition debugging (F12)"
dialogues.setConditionDebuggingF12.tip = In-game, pressing F12 logs a pass/fail breakdown of this dialogue's start conditions (with current variable values) to the BepInEx console. Diagnostic only — no gameplay effect.

# Tooltip on a text box
dialogues.whenSeveralDialoguesBecome.tip = When several dialogues become eligible on the same tick, the highest priority starts first (ties: manifest order). Default 0.

# Tooltip on "+ Add group"
dialogues.addDialogueStartConditionGroup.tip = Add an AND/OR group to bundle conditions like parentheses

# Tooltip on "Copy all"
dialogues.copyStartConditions.tip = Copy these start conditions (except the pinned level gate) to the clipboard

# Tooltip on "Paste"
dialogues.pasteStartConditions.tip = Append clipboard conditions

# Tooltip on "Overwrite"
dialogues.overwriteStartConditions.tip = Replace these start conditions with the clipboard (keeps the pinned level gate)

# Tooltip on a toggle button
dialogues.gameLookSwitch.tip = Draw each line in the list the way the game draws it — its own font, outline and drop shadow, the speaker's name in their colour, on a dark panel. Costs the spelling squiggles on those rows; they stay in the Text box below the list.

# Tooltip on "+ Root"
dialogues.addDialogueRootNode.tip = Add a new top-level node (no parent). It takes the speaker from the line it follows - actor, expression and outfit - and nothing else, because a root starts a fresh strand.

# Tooltip on "+ Child"
dialogues.addDialogueChildNode.tip = Add a child of the currently-selected node, copying its actor, expression, outfit, conditions and actions — everything but the text and the jump tag.

# Tooltip on "+ Sibling"
dialogues.addDialogueSiblingNode.tip = Add a node directly below the selected one, sharing its parent (or another root when the selection is a root). Copies everything but the text and the jump tag.

# Tooltip on "− Remove"
dialogues.removeDialogue.tip3 = Delete the selected node. References to it from other nodes' children + the root list are scrubbed automatically.

# Keyboard shortcut shown beside it ("Copy node (+ children)")
dialogues.copyNode.keys = Ctrl+C

# Keyboard shortcut shown beside it ("Paste below (sibling)")
dialogues.pasteNodeSibling.keys = Ctrl+V

# Tooltip on "Paste below (sibling)"
dialogues.pasteNodeSibling.tip = Paste the copied subtree directly under the selected node, sharing its parent.

# Tooltip on a dropdown
dialogues.nodeKind.tip = Text plays a line. Choice offers this node's children and lets the player pick one. Random picks one of them itself.

# Tooltip on "Choice Option"
dialogues.choiceOption.tip = This node is one of a Choice's options — the button a player picks. Its kind is decided by its parent.

# Tooltip on a dropdown
dialogues.nodeActorPicker.tip = Who speaks this line, and every line after it until another node changes it. A character's runtime name; the line is labelled with that character's display name.

# Tooltip on a dropdown
dialogues.whichExpressionSpeakerWears.tip = Which expression the speaker wears from this node onward.

# Tooltip on a dropdown
dialogues.switchActorOneTheir.tip = Switch the actor to one of their outfits for this node onward. Pick an outfit, or type $variableName to use the outfit named by a pack variable's current value (so this node chooses the outfit dynamically). Leave blank to keep the actor's current bust.

# Tooltip on a button
dialogues.markupBold.tip = Bold  (Ctrl+B) — wraps the selected words in <b></b>, or opens an empty pair where the cursor is.

# Tooltip on a button
dialogues.markupItalic.tip = Italic  (Ctrl+I) — wraps the selected words in <i></i>, or opens an empty pair where the cursor is.

# Tooltip on a button
dialogues.markupColor.tip = Colour  (Ctrl+Shift+C) — pick a colour, and the selected words are wrapped in <color=#RRGGBB></color>, or an empty pair opens where the cursor is. The value can be edited in place; the words take the colour as you type it.

# Tooltip on a button
dialogues.markupSize.tip = Size  (Ctrl+Shift+S) — wraps the selection in <size=70%></size>. The one tag the game uses in its own lines, for a muttered aside.

# Tooltip on the button over a dialogue line that lists the pack's auto-trigger patterns
dialogues.sfxPattern.tip = Sound cue - pick one of the pack's auto-trigger patterns to write it into the line where the cursor is. When the line shows it, its sound plays by itself.

# The only row of that list when the pack has no auto-trigger patterns
dialogues.sfxPattern.none = No auto-trigger patterns yet - add them to a sound in the SFX tab.

# Tooltip on a row of that list; {sound} is the sound's name
dialogues.sfxPattern.item.tip = Write this into the line - it plays {sound}.

# Tooltip on the play button on a row of that list; {sound} is the sound's name
dialogues.sfxPattern.play.tip = Play {sound}

# Under a line's text: the line reads exactly as written in every language, and is never translated
dialogues.textSameEverywhere = Same in every language

# Tooltip on that tick
dialogues.textSameEverywhere.tip = The line reads exactly as written in every language. It is never offered for translating, and the game shows it as written whatever language the player picked. For a sound, a name on its own, or anything that must not change.

# Under a line's text: its words are never translated, only written in another alphabet's letters where a language has one
dialogues.textLettersOnly = Keep the words, change only the letters

# Tooltip on that tick
dialogues.textLettersOnly.tip = For a made-up language, a spell or a chant: the words are never translated, so they cannot be turned into real words they happen to look like. In a language written in the same letters the line reads as written; in one with an alphabet of its own, like Russian or Japanese, each word is spelled out in its letters, so players can read it aloud.

# Tooltip on a part of the window
dialogues.nodeTextBox.tip = The line itself. [PV:name] is replaced with a pack variable. {PC} is the player's name, and {M} {D} {B} are what this player calls their mother, father and brother — write those rather than the words, because players choose them. Markup is shown as the player will see it: the tags are set apart in their own colour and the words they wrap take their formatting. The game’s own tokens — {PC} and the rest — are marked the same way, in place, because a name goes there rather than those characters.

# Under a line's Text box. {size} is the size the game shrinks the line to, from its usual 38.
dialogues.fit.shrinks = In game this line is shrunk to {size} pt to fit the dialogue box.

dialogues.fit.spills = Too long for the dialogue box: even at its smallest size it runs out of the box in game. Split it into two lines.

# Tooltip on a text box
dialogues.optionalOtherNodesCan.tip = Optional. Other nodes can use Jump → tag to resume from here.

# Tooltip on a dropdown
dialogues.wherePlayGoesAfter.tip = Where play goes after this node. Continue moves to the next node down the list, whether or not it is a child. Exit stops the conversation. Jump resumes at the node carrying the tag named below.

# Tooltip on a dropdown
dialogues.whichTaggedNodeResume.tip = Which tagged node to resume at. It has to match that node's Tag exactly; a tag that does not exist fails silently at runtime, so pick from the list where you can.

# Tooltip on a dropdown
dialogues.howTextLineAdvances.tip = How a text line advances. UntilInteraction waits for the player; Timeout auto-advances after the typewriter finishes.

# Tooltip on a text box
dialogues.secondsLineLingersAfter.tip = Seconds the line lingers after typing finishes. Decimals OK, e.g. 1.5.

# Tooltip on "Copy all"
dialogues.copyStartConditions.tip2 = Copy all conditions here to the clipboard

# Tooltip on "Overwrite"
dialogues.overwriteStartConditions.tip2 = Replace all conditions here with the clipboard

# Tooltip on "Copy all"
dialogues.copyStartConditions.tip3 = Copy these actions to the clipboard

# Tooltip on "Paste"
dialogues.pasteStartConditions.tip2 = Append clipboard actions

# Tooltip on "Overwrite"
dialogues.overwriteStartConditions.tip3 = Replace these actions with the clipboard

# Tooltip on a reset button; {0} is the part of the line it resets
dialogues.resetLinePart.tip = Put this line's {0} back the way the game has it

# Tooltip on a line's tag; {0} is the tag
dialogues.jumpTag.tip = Jump target — tag: {0}

[SFX tab]

# Tab
sfx.sfx = SFX

# Button

# Box heading

# Text

# Box heading
sfx.audio = Audio

# Text
sfx.packRelativePathBase = Pack-relative path to the base clip. Variants are auto-detected — drop <basename>_1.<ext>, <basename>_2.<ext>, … beside the base and the registry picks one at random per play. OGG / WAV / MP3 are all accepted (the variant can have a different extension than the base).

# Text
sfx.audioPath = Audio path:

# Text
sfx.defaultVolume = Default volume:

# Button
sfx.playSfx = ▶ Play

# Button
sfx.stopSfx = ■ Stop

# Text
sfx.playsThroughEditorS = Plays through the editor's own audio (OGG/WAV/MP3).

# Box heading
sfx.autoTriggerPatterns = Auto-trigger patterns

# Text
sfx.commaSeparatedListText = Comma-separated list of text patterns. When any pattern appears in a dialogue node's text on node-start, the SFX fires automatically. If the effect has variants, one is picked at random each time — add them by putting files named <name>_1, <name>_2, … beside the base file (numbered without gaps). Convention: asterisk-bracketed lowercase tokens like *plap*, *smooch*. One SFX can carry multiple patterns — *yank*,*yeet* both fire the same clip just like the host mod does. Leave blank for an SFX that only plays via explicit PlaySFX actions.

# Tooltip on "+ Folder"
sfx.unitAddFolder.tip = Add a folder. Drag items/folders to organize (cosmetic — doesn't change the pack). Rename a folder by right-clicking it, or select it and press F2.

# Tooltip on "− Remove"
sfx.removeSfx.tip = Remove the selected SFX, or delete the selected folder (its contents move up).

# Tooltip on a tree
sfx.sfxTreeView.tip = Ctrl+click / Shift+click selects extra items to drag into a folder together. The detail pane follows the normally-selected item.

# Tooltip on a text box
sfx.whatCalledEditorS.tip = What this is called in the editor's own lists, and the only name you need to write — the runtime name beside it follows along until you set one yourself.

# Tooltip on a text box
sfx.namePackItselfUses.tip = The name the pack itself uses — what the runtime calls this in its log, what validation messages name, and what another pack would reference it by. It is written for you from the display name, so there is nothing to fill in; it is shown because those three places quote it back at you. Select it to copy.

# Tooltip on a part of the window
sfx.audioFileRelativePack.tip = The audio file, relative to the pack folder. OGG, WAV and MP3 all work. Extra recordings named _1, _2 beside it are picked between at random.

# Tooltip on a text box
sfx.01UsedPlaysfx.tip = How loud the sound plays when PlaySFX does not say, and when an auto-trigger pattern plays it: 1.0 is the game's own level, 0 is silent, and up to 5 is louder than the game goes by itself. The game squares it on the way out, as it does with every sound it plays this way - 0.5 is a quarter, 2 is four times - and Play sounds it the same.

# Tooltip on "▶ Play"
sfx.playSfx.tip = Preview the sound as loud as the game plays it, at the default volume above - as edited below, when it is. Needs the pack saved to disk.

# Tooltip on a text box
sfx.wordsFireEffectTheir.tip = Words that fire this effect on their own when they appear in a line, comma-separated. The convention is a lowercase word between asterisks, like *plap*. Leave it empty for an effect only an action should play.

# Heading of the box on the SFX tab where a sound is cut and given effects
sfxEdit.header = Edit the sound

# Small print at the top of that box
sfxEdit.help = Click the strip to pick a spot; the mouse wheel zooms in and out, and Shift with the wheel scrolls. Cut here splits the piece at the spot in two, Delete piece takes the highlighted piece out. When the pack is saved, the edited sound is written over the file, and the recording is kept in the pack's .originals folder, which is never exported - so Reset always gives it back.

# Label before a list of the sound's files: the sound and its variants
sfxEdit.file = File:

# Tooltip on that list
sfxEdit.file.tip = This sound has variants the game picks between at random. Each is cut on its own; pitch, speed, echo and reverb apply to all of them.

# Tooltip on the strip showing the sound's waveform
sfxEdit.strip.tip = Click to pick a spot. Mouse wheel: zoom in or out. Shift + wheel: scroll. Delete: take the highlighted piece out.

# Button
sfxEdit.play = ▶ Play

# Tooltip on "▶ Play"
sfxEdit.play.tip = Play the sound as edited, from the spot picked.

# Button
sfxEdit.stop = ■ Stop

# Button: splits the piece of the sound at the picked spot into two pieces
sfxEdit.split = ✂ Cut here

# Tooltip on "✂ Cut here"
sfxEdit.split.tip = Split the piece at the spot picked into two pieces, so either can be taken out or kept. The spot has to be inside a piece, not on its edge.

# Button: removes the highlighted piece of the sound
sfxEdit.delete = Delete piece

# Tooltip on "Delete piece"
sfxEdit.delete.tip = Take the highlighted piece out; the pieces after it move up to close the gap. The last piece left cannot be deleted.

# Button: zoom in on the strip
sfxEdit.zoomIn = +

# Tooltip on "+"
sfxEdit.zoomIn.tip = Zoom in. The mouse wheel over the strip does the same.

# Button: zoom out on the strip
sfxEdit.zoomOut = −

# Tooltip on "−"
sfxEdit.zoomOut.tip = Zoom out.

# Button: zoom so the whole sound fits
sfxEdit.fit = Fit

# Tooltip on "Fit"
sfxEdit.fit.tip = Show the whole sound.

# Button: undo every edit of the sound
sfxEdit.reset = Reset

# Tooltip on "Reset"
sfxEdit.reset.tip = Put the sound back as recorded: every cut in every file and every effect goes, and the recording plays again. Undo brings the edit back.

# A line under the strip. {cursor}, {length} and {was} are seconds
sfxEdit.summary = Spot {cursor} s · piece {piece} of {pieces} · {length} s long (recorded: {was} s)

# Label beside a slider
sfxEdit.pitch = Pitch

# Tooltip on the Pitch slider
sfxEdit.pitch.tip = Higher or lower, in semitones, without changing how long the sound lasts.

# Label beside a slider
sfxEdit.speed = Speed

# Tooltip on the Speed slider
sfxEdit.speed.tip = Faster or slower, without changing how high the sound is.

# Label beside a slider
sfxEdit.echo = Echo

# Tooltip on the Echo slider
sfxEdit.echo.tip = How loud each repeat is. Off at the far left.

# Label beside a slider
sfxEdit.echoDelay = Echo delay

# Tooltip on the Echo delay slider
sfxEdit.echoDelay.tip = The time between the sound and each repeat. Set the echo above first.

# Label beside a slider
sfxEdit.reverb = Reverb

# Tooltip on the Reverb slider
sfxEdit.reverb.tip = How much of the room is heard. Off at the far left.

# Label beside a slider
sfxEdit.room = Room size

# Tooltip on the Room size slider
sfxEdit.room.tip = From a small room to a hall. Set the reverb above first.

# Button
sfxEdit.resetEffects = Reset effects

# Tooltip on "Reset effects"
sfxEdit.resetEffects.tip = Put pitch, speed, echo and reverb back as recorded, keeping the cuts.

# Beside the Pitch or Speed slider when it is unchanged
sfxEdit.asRecorded = as recorded

# Beside the Echo or Reverb slider when it is at zero
sfxEdit.off = off

# Beside the Pitch slider. {n} is a number with its sign, like +2 or -1.5
sfxEdit.semitones = {n} semitones

# Beside the Pitch slider, for exactly one semitone up or down. {n} is +1 or -1
sfxEdit.semitone = {n} semitone

# Beside the Echo, Reverb and Room size sliders: a percentage. {n} is the number
sfxEdit.percent = {n}%

# Beside the Echo delay slider: a time. {n} is the number of seconds
sfxEdit.seconds = {n} s

# On the strip, when the sound has no file yet
sfxEdit.noFile = Pick an audio file above to edit it here.

# On the strip, when the pack has never been saved
sfxEdit.saveFirst = Save the pack first: the sound is read from the pack's folder.

# On the strip, when the sound's file is not there
sfxEdit.missing = The file '{file}' isn't in the pack's folder.

# On the strip, while the sound is read
sfxEdit.reading = Reading the sound…

# Under the sound editor's strip, while a long sound - a music track - is being made with its edits before it plays
sfxEdit.preparing = Preparing the sound to play…

# On the strip, when the file is not a sound that can be read
sfxEdit.unreadable = '{file}' could not be read as audio.

# Message after saving, when an edited sound could not be made into its file
sfxEdit.renderFailed = An edited sound could not be made: {error}. The rest of the pack was saved, and the sound plays as it did before.

LevelRandom = re-roll on every entry to the level picked below (gates on that level's activeSelf in vanilla).

# Tooltip on a dropdown

# Tooltip on a text box

# Tooltip on a text box

# Tooltip on a text box

# Tab
integration.integration = Integration

# Heading
integration.updateRules = Update rules

# Button
integration.addIntegrationRule = + Rule

# Button
integration.addIntegrationFolder = + Folder

# Button
integration.removeIntegrationRule = − Remove

# Box heading
integration.identity = Identity

# Text
integration.displayName = Display name:

# Text
integration.runtimeName = Runtime name:

# Text
integration.description = Description:

# Text
integration.triggerMode = Trigger mode:

# Tick box
integration.ruleDebug = Set for condition debugging

# Box heading
integration.repeatEachOptional = Repeat for each (optional)

# Text
integration.runsWholeRuleOnce = Runs this whole rule once per value, so one rule can cover a set of subjects instead of copying it per subject. Values: a comma-separated list (A,B,C), or $variableName naming a List variable (or a String variable holding CSV) — so the set can change at runtime. Each value is substituted wherever {name} appears in any condition or action, including branches: e.g. Location_{char} as a variable name, or {char}/Slot as a GameObject path. It composes with the rest of the syntax — $Prefix_{char} dereferences the per-value variable. Every value gets its own edge state and its own Timer deadlines, so values behave exactly like the separate rules they replace (tick 'stagger' on a Timer to spread their first fire apart). Leave blank to run the rule once.

# Text
integration.eachValue = For each value in:

# Text
integration.placeholderName = Placeholder name:

# Box heading
integration.editingMode = Editing mode

# Tick box
integration.codeModeEditConditions = Code mode (edit conditions + actions as JSON)

# Text
integration.pickerModePickAction = Picker mode = pick from the action / condition catalogs row-by-row. Code mode = author the rule body directly as JSON. Same data, two views.

# Box heading
integration.ruleConditions = Conditions (all must pass)

# Button
integration.addIntegrationCondition = + Add condition

integration.copyConditions = Copy all

integration.pasteConditions = Paste

integration.overwriteConditions = Overwrite

integration.ruleActionsBox = Actions (fired when conditions trigger)

# Button
integration.addIntegrationAction = + Add action

# Box heading
integration.elseIfBranchesFirst = Else-if branches (first match wins)

# Button
integration.addBranch = + Add else-if branch

# Text
integration.ifRuleSMain = If the rule's main conditions fail, these branches are tried top to bottom and the FIRST one whose conditions pass fires its actions. Edge trigger modes fire whenever the winning branch changes — ideal for schedule-style cascades (if slot A → …, else if slot B → …, else → …).

# Box heading
integration.ruleBodyJSON = Rule body (JSON)

# Text
integration.editRuleSTrigger = Edit the rule's trigger mode + conditions + actions as a JSON object. Format: { "triggerMode": "OnRisingEdge", "conditions": [...], "actions": [...], "branches": [ { "conditions": [...], "actions": [...] } ] }. branches is the optional else-if chain (first match wins; empty conditions = plain Else). The conditions / actions arrays carry the same shape as dialogue nodes' actionsOnStart.

# Tooltip on "+ Folder"
integration.addIntegrationFolder.tip = Add a folder. Drag rules/folders to organize (cosmetic — doesn't change when rules run). Rename a folder by right-clicking it, or select it and press F2.

# Tooltip on "− Remove"
integration.removeIntegrationRule.tip = Remove the selected rule, or delete the selected folder (its contents move up).

# Tooltip on a tree
integration.integrationTreeView.tip = Ctrl+click / Shift+click selects extra items to drag into a folder together. The detail pane follows the normally-selected item.

# Tooltip on a text box
integration.whatCalledEditorS.tip = What this is called in the editor's own lists, and the only name you need to write — the runtime name beside it follows along until you set one yourself.

# Tooltip on "Runtime name:"
integration.runtimeName.tip = Stable identifier — used in logs and for the rule's edge tracking. Filled in from the display name while you have not set one yourself; type here and it stops following, so an established rule is never renamed underneath whatever already points at it.

# Tooltip on a text box
integration.namePackItselfUses.tip = The name the pack itself uses — what the runtime calls this in its log, what validation messages name, and what another pack would reference it by. It is written for you from the display name, so there is nothing to fill in; it is shown because those three places quote it back at you. Select it to copy.

# Tooltip on a text box
integration.freeTextShownEditor.tip = Free text — shown in the editor only, not at runtime. Useful for noting why a rule exists.

# Tooltip on a dropdown
integration.triggerMode.tip = OnRisingEdge = fires once when conditions become true. OnFallingEdge = fires once when they stop. WhilePassing = fires every frame conditions match. OnSceneLoad / OnDayChange = one-shot per lifecycle event.

# Tooltip on "Set for condition debugging"
integration.ruleDebug.tip = Log what this rule decides to the BepInEx console: which branch won, which won last tick, and whether it fired — plus a per-condition breakdown with live values whenever no branch passes. Logged only when the decision changes, so it won't flood. Diagnostic only; no gameplay effect.

# Tooltip on a dropdown
integration.listVariableNameLiteral.tip = A List variable as $name, or a literal comma-separated list. Blank = no repetition.

# Tooltip on a text box
integration.whatWriteInsideBraces.tip = What to write inside braces in the conditions/actions. Defaults to 'item', i.e. {item}. Set it to 'char' to write {char}.

# Tooltip on "Code mode (edit conditions + actions as JSON)"
integration.codeModeEditConditions.tip = When checked, the picker rows below are replaced with a JSON textbox. The textbox round-trips losslessly with the picker — toggling between modes preserves all data. Parse errors keep the toggle in code mode until you fix the JSON.

# Tooltip on "+ Add group"
integration.addIntegrationConditionGroup.tip = Add an AND/OR group to bundle conditions like parentheses

# Tooltip on "Copy all"
integration.copyConditions.tip = Copy all conditions here to the clipboard

# Tooltip on "Paste"
integration.pasteConditions.tip = Append clipboard conditions (works across dialogues, rules and level hooks)

# Tooltip on "Overwrite"
integration.overwriteConditions.tip = Replace all conditions here with the clipboard

# Tooltip on "Copy all"
integration.copyConditions.tip2 = Copy these actions to the clipboard

# Tooltip on "Paste"
integration.pasteConditions.tip2 = Append clipboard actions (works across dialogues, rules and level hooks)

# Tooltip on "Overwrite"
integration.overwriteConditions.tip2 = Replace these actions with the clipboard

# Tooltip on "+ Add else-if branch"
integration.addBranch.tip = Add a branch tried when the conditions above (and every earlier branch) fail. Leave its conditions empty to make it a plain Else.

# Tooltip on a text box
integration.ruleSConditionsActions.tip = This rule's conditions and actions as JSON. The two views round-trip, so you can move between them freely; a parse error keeps you here until it is fixed.

# Tooltip on a dropdown
ui.textmeshproFontAssetNot.tip = A TextMeshPro font asset, not a font file. The same typeface appears more than once with different outlines and shadows, and they are not interchangeable.

# Text

Only a task with no subtasks has completion conditions and actions - a task with subtasks finishes through them. A dialogue or a rule can still drive a quest with the Quest action, and check on it with the QuestState and QuestCounter conditions. Completing a quest gives the player the same reward the game's own quests do.
